Advances in forecasting models and alpha discovery help identify opportunities. However, traditional pipelines often decouple signal forecasting from portfolio construction, potentially misaligning learning with allocation and execution objectives. We present AllocFlow, an end-to-end flow-matching framework that directly generates joint portfolio allocations from raw multi-asset market histories. To capture dependencies among portfolio positions, AllocFlow employs cross-asset flow Transformer conditioned on temporal market representations to generate continuous, signed position weights across all instruments jointly. To address the sample inefficiency and instability of learning continuous allocation policies from scratch, we establish two-stage training scheme: strategy-supervised imitation shapes structured distribution of candidate portfolios, followed by group-relative reinforcement learning (RL) that refines joint decisions using portfolio-level financial rewards and frozen policy reference. On hourly cryptocurrency perpetual futures, RL refinement raises the annualized net return of the imitation policy from 27.79% to 51.38% and its Calmar ratio from 0.955 to 4.043, surpassing the teacher strategy on every metric, and AllocFlow reaches Calmar ratio of 4.90 on six further months of unseen 2026 data without retraining. Text:"IFM Technical Report: 2026-11-16 K2-Horizon: Frontier Fleet of Open Source Language Models K2 Team, Institute of Foundation Models* We present K2-Horizon, fleet of six fully open language models, from 0.9B dense model to 375B-A23B MoE, built for general-purpose reasoning and agentic work across math and science, software engineering, deep research, office automation, and beyond. The models lead or are competitive with strong open models of their size on agentic and coding benchmarks. On the modeling side, to strengthen reasoning, we introduce Mixture-of-Value Attention (MoVA), applying MoE to attention value projections, and softplus attention gating, more flexible, theoretically motivated gate. Our 36B-A4B model uses both and nearly matches 32B dense model trained on the same data, with one-seventh the active parameters. We also introduce Uno, lossless diffusion adapters that speed up decoding by up to 2.2 without separate draft model. Our release follows the LLM360 definition of open source for transparency and reproducibility: the full development history, including intermediate checkpoints from every training stage, pre-training data, training logs, and all training, evaluation, and infrastructure code, notably our production-scale xLLM framework for dense and MoE training and the RL360 reinforcement-learning stack. Beyond the data itself, we release and document in detail every pipeline that built it, the part of model development that open releases most rarely disclose. These pipelines produced roughly 10T synthetic pre-training tokens, showing that synthetic data can be major ingredient of pre-training at scale. Four of the models share one 22T-token pre-training corpus and recipe, revealing consistent scaling behavior and enabling controlled studies of scale and sparsity. All artifacts are available at https://ifm.ai/k2/. Correspondence to: {hector.liu, eric.xing}@mbzuai.ac.ae Released Artifacts K2-Horizon is released together with the artifacts used to buil"

Text:"InclusionMed: Benchmarking AI for Inclusive Healthcareinfo@inclusionmed.ai (cid:263) Email (cid:231) Website https://inclusionmed.ai/ (cid:97) GitHub https://github.com/panuvi/inclusionmedAdvancing medical AI for inclusive healthcare requires evaluation across diverse healthcare roles, tasks, and care settings, but benchmark coverage remains uneven. We introduce InclusionMed, an open medical AI evaluation initiative for inclusive healthcare. The InclusionMed Atlas organizes benchmarks across three healthcare roles and three AI capabilities. For the first release, we selected four public benchmarks from 388 candidates through structured audits and independent reproduction. With InclusionMed-DxFiles, our private diagnostic reasoning benchmark, the Atlas contains five benchmarks covering three of its nine rolecapability cells. We evaluated frontier large language models on the Atlas and aggregated their results into the InclusionMed Index, with overall scores ranging from 17.5 to 49.1 out of 100. The Index also reports scores by healthcare role and AI capability, showing that models rank varied with the role or capability considered. within-benchmark analysis using task-level tags showed that the ten highest-ranked models in the Index all scored higher on patient communication than on clinical decision-making. Models with similar Index scores also differed substantially in estimated inference cost. These findings highlight the need for evaluation covering more diverse healthcare needs and for context-specific model selection. Through open collaboration and community contributions, InclusionMed seeks to ensure that diverse healthcare needs worldwide shape how medical AI progress is defined and measured. Figure 1 InclusionMed Atlas. Benchmarks are organized by healthcare role (rows) and AI capability (columns). Dark blue tiles mark the four public benchmarks and the private InclusionMed-DxFiles (), each contributing 20% to the Index. Light blue tiles show candidates excluded for score saturation (*) or limited discrimination () in this evaluation. Hatched cells indicate no eligible benchmark for independent reproduction in this release. Technical Report Version 0.5 2026 InclusionMed. Text:"SWE-CoWork: Benchmarking Coding Agents as Digital Employees in Organizations TokenWave AI1 coding agent working within software organization must establish what to build, collaborate with colleagues, and verify what it delivers. We introduce SWE-CoWork, benchmark comprising 101 development assignments across 101 real repositories, 11 programming languages, and 12 application domains. Each assignment places an agent in simulated engineering organization with 26 colleagues who have distinct responsibilities, bounded knowledge, and controlled collaboration behavior. Starting from brief handoff, agents must recover requirements distributed across colleagues and workplace records, reconcile conflicting evidence, and implement changes evaluated by private executable tests. We evaluate eleven models and find substantial gap between partial implementation and complete task delivery. The two leading models satisfy approximately 63% of fail-to-pass (F2P) tests but complete fewer than 8% of assignments. Providing complete requirements upfront improves F2P by approximately 16 percentage points for Seed Evolving and GPT-6 Astra, yet leaves complete task success unchanged. Requirement-level trajectory analysis reveals failures throughout the delivery process: Claude-Opus-5.5 directs mean of 93% of first questions about queried cards to their accountable owners across runs but obtains only 64% of colleague-held requirements. Deferred questions go unfollowed, implementations remain incomplete even after requirements are acquired, and 31% of requirements reported as completed fail their tests. These findings highlight sustained requirement tracking, from clarification through implementation, verification, and handoff, as key challenge for reliable autonomous software engineering. Date: November 17, 2026 Website: https://tokenwave-ai.github.io/swe-cowork-page Code: https://github.com/TokenWave-AI/swe-cowork-bench Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Tokenwave-AI/swe-cowork Author"

We introduce Accelerate Bench, benchmark of 32 CPU optimization tasks adapted from scientific applications and supercomputing competitions. Each task provides working program and specifies the workload, correctness requirements, timing boundaries, and permitted edits, leaving agents to choose where and how to optimize. Public testing supports iterative development, and separate verifier scores the final submission. On benchmark version 1.1, we evaluate 16 coding agents, each pairing model with harness. Their Score@1 ranges from 25.5% to 67.4%. Most of their programs are correct, and the size of the speedup separates them: the two leading agents are at least 2 faster on 22 or more of the 32 tasks, while the other fourteen reach 2 on 8 to 17 and leave 125 of their 412 correct outcomes below 1.25. Controlled replays show why coordination across an application matters: identical source code runs between 0.29 and 2.12 depending only on build and runtime settings, and one code change is worth 1.01 or 6.4 depending on another. Accelerate Bench pairs executable workloads with agents programs and trajectories to evaluate both their gains and the process behind them. 1. Text:"Latent Feedback Is Compute and Data Amplifier Scaling Law for Latent Feedback Transformers on Repeated Data Benhao Huang, Chaitanya Dwivedi, Xuezhe Ma, Alexander Moreno, Hector Liu Institute of Foundation Models, USC, (cid:97) Code: https://github.com/ifm-ai/LoRM Date: November 11, 2026 Overview When high-quality data runs out, common practice is to train on it for several epochs, but each repeat is worth less than the last. Latent feedback, which feeds each tokens top-layer state into the next tokens input, is another way to spend that compute, yet it has not been tested against vanilla Transformer that spends the same compute on more epochs. We run this test across ladder of model sizes, unique data and epochs, tuning both models at every size and amount of unique data. Latent feedback makes every FLOP and every token count for more except when unique data is scarce relative to model size, regime that todays models are far from. We find that latent feedback serves as an amplifier: with unique data fixed, it reaches Transformers loss with less compute; with epochs fixed, it reaches that loss with less unique data. Furthermore, both savings grow with unique data per parameter and with repetition, and downstream tasks agree. To go beyond our grid, we fit one scaling law to both models, in which latent feedback enters as Transformer with more parameters; it predicts that both savings keep growing with unique data and repetition. Fitted on models from 60M to 271M parameters, the law predicts the losses at held-out 778M size to within 0.3% and the gap between the two models to within 0.05%. At most, latent feedback saves 48% of the compute and 47% of the unique data. When data, not compute, is the limit, latent feedback is the better use of FLOP for model of given size. Figure 1: Latent feedback saves compute and unique data. (a) Latent feedback: each tokens top-layer state enters the next tokens input through map W. (b) The share of compute that latent feedback saves a"

Text:"LoopFill: Pipelined Multi-Token Speculative Decoding for Looped Transformers Siqi Zhu 1 Suozhi Huang 2 Yi Zhao 1 Jiaxuan YouLooped Transformers improve parameter efficiency by repeatedly applying the same Transformer layers. During autoregressive decoding, however, generating each token requires several sequential passes through these layers, leading to high decoding latency. Pipelined self-speculative methods use shallow-loop predictions to process tokens at different loops in the same batch, but each token must reach the drafting depth before the next can enter the pipeline. We introduce LoopFill, training-free speculative decoding algorithm for looped LLM that combines multitoken suffix drafts with shallow-loop predictions in single draft tree. Both drafters contribute to one tree, so suffix lookup can immediately extend shallow prediction and several future tokens can enter the pipeline together. The shared block computes the highest-scoring draft candidates at different loop depths in one batch, and full-depth verification preserves the models autoregressive output distribution. Across seven benchmarks, LoopFills average speedup over greedy autoregressive decoding is 5.85 on Ouro2.6B and 5.13 on Huginn-3.5B. The speedups exceed those of LoopAhead, the strongest baseline, by 40% on Ouro-2.6B and 25% on Huginn3.5B. 1. Introduction Looped Transformer is parameter-efficient architecture for reasoning language models (Dehghani et al., 2019; Geiping et al., 2025a; Zhu et al., 2026; Bae et al., 2025). They usually consist of prelude, weight-shared block applied times, and coda (Figure 2(a)). Iterating the block refines the hidden state without emitting tokens, favoring latent reasoning (Saunshi et al., 2025): Huginn-3.5B improves as its test-time recurrence grows (Geiping et al., 2025a), and the 1.4B and 2.6B Ouro models match larger standard 1University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign 2Princeton University. Correspondence to: Siqi Zhu <siqizhu4@illinois.edu>. Preprint. November 17, 2026. 1 LLMs (Zhu et al., 2026). The price is slow decoding. Each token requires passes through the block, and at small batch sizes each pass is memory-bound (Pope et al., 2022), leaving most of the compute idle. LoopSpec (Cho et al., 2026) and WaveFront Decoding (Ha & Kim, 2026) turn this recurrence into speculative decoding pipeline. After < loops, the output head reads draft token from the hidden state, and the next token starts from this draft while the current one continues to depth to verify it. Since the loops share weights, positions at different depths run in one batched pass. This pipeline relies on an inductive bias of looped models, which are trained to predict at varying depths (Geiping et al., 2025a; Zhu et al., 2026): shallow-loop predictions agree closely with full-depth ones. On Ouro-2.6B, the top-1 token after one of = 4 loops matches the full-depth greedy token at 88.8% of positions, and after two loops at 95.6% (Figure 1(a)). Figure 1. Loop-d and suffix-tree drafts on Ouro-2.6B. (a) Blue: how often the loop-d draft equals the full-depth output. Orange: additional loop 2 hits where loop 1 misses, and additional loop 3 hits where loops 1 and 2 both miss. (b) Mean length of the correct draft for suffix drafts alone, adding loop-1 draft (+Shallow), and also adding suffix draft after loop-1 draft (+Requery) However, these methods draft only along the recurrent depth. Each shallow prediction proposes one token, so new token starts at most once every loop steps and the speedup is bounded by /d. rejected draft further forces restart. These bubbles leave the pipeline underfilled, while the sequence dimension, i.e., starting several future tokens together, stays unused. LoopAhead (Liu et al., 2026) adds multi-token prediction using trainable heads  (Table 1)  . We find that training-free drafter suffices. suffix tree over the prompt and previous outputs (Oliaro et al., 2025) retrieves multi-token continuations without model computaLoopFill: Pipelined Multi-Token Speculative Decoding for Looped Transformers Table 1. Comparison with existing speculative decoding methods from four perspectives. LoopFill combines multi-token suffix drafts with shallow-loop draft from the target model in draft tree without training. Method Data-driven drafting SuffixDecoding (Oliaro et al., 2025) + EAGLE-3 (Li et al., 2025) Model-driven drafting LoopSpec (Cho et al., 2026) WaveFront (Ha & Kim, 2026) LoopAhead (Liu et al., 2026) DFlash (Chen et al., 2026) LoopFill Data Model Multi-token Training-free tion. Combined with shallow drafts, it extends the correct draft path on Ouro-2.6B from 1.34 to 1.73 tokens, and to 2.28 tokens when the suffix tree is also queried at the loop-1 draft (Figure 1(b)). The pairing is natural: shallow-loop drafting shortens the wait between the starts of adjacent tokens, while suffix-tree drafting proposes several future tokens without waiting for any loop. We propose LoopFill, which coordinates the two drafters in unified draft tree (Figure 3). Both drafters attach candidates to the same tree, so suffix continuation can follow shallow draft and vice versa, and token proposed by both shares one node. Every draft node occupies row of the batched block at every loop, so discarded drafts take compute from other requests. LoopFill therefore scores each node by the product of shallow-prediction probabilities and suffix frequencies along its path, computes only the top-N nodes in each loop step, and prunes branches that later shallow prediction rules out. When the root completes loops, the matching subtree is kept together with its completed loops. Drafts decide only which positions start early, never which token is emitted, so LoopFill preserves the output of autoregressive decoding. The best per-request draft budget depends on hardware and workload: On Huginn-3.5B, increasing the draft node budget from = 16 to = 64 raises the decoding speed by 19.5% at = 1 and reduces end-to-end throughput by 43% at = 16 (Figure 5). Our contributions are as follows: We propose LoopFill, training-free, pipelined multitoken speculative decoding method for looped language models. It combines data-driven suffix-tree drafter with the models own shallow-loop drafts to fill the bubbles of the loop pipeline, while preserving autoregressive outputs. We design dual-drafter draft tree that shares computation, verification, and reuse between the two drafters, with path-score pruning under node budget. On seven benchmarks, "

Offline grasp generators such as Contact-GraspNet work this way, but they rank grasps by learned plausibility rather than by whether the grasp actually lifts the object. Reinforcement learning (RL) can optimize for that directly and make point-cloud grasping policy much better on average, yet it leaves specific grasps persistently unsolved. We present human-in-the-loop failure loop that targets those grasps: situations an RL policy keeps failing are mined automatically, sent to virtual-reality application where person picks the object up with their own hands, and returned to training as two grasp points. The person contributes only where to grasp; the robot chooses its own approach. In simulation, RL raises success on unseen objects from 55% with Contact-GraspNet to 83%. Adding 58 human demonstrations nearly doubles success on the hard cases RL kept failing, from 24% to 45%, and recovers performance that RL alone loses on similar cases that were never demonstrated, without reducing success on unseen objects. I. INTRODUCTION Grasping policies operating in point-cloud space work without category label or classifier; instead, they operate on learned geometric features that tend to lead to success. We therefore introduce UMM-JEPA, which reformulates generation as progressive prediction of continuous visual-understanding representations. Text:"GlyphAtlas: Disentangling Stroke Detail from Layout for Scene Text Synthesis Luozheng Qin1,* Changhai Zhou2,* Yiyi Zhou1,# 1Xiamen University * Equal contribution 2Fudan University # Corresponding author Figure 1: GlyphAtlas renders stroke-accurate text that blends seamlessly into real scenes. It keeps every text region correct in dense, multi-line layouts, harmonizes typography with scene geometry and lighting, stays legible when glyphs are small, dense, or structurecomplex, and generalizes to scripts unseen during training. Abstract Scene text synthesis requires stroke-accurate characters rendered in specified layout, as well as visual coherence with the surrounding scene. To achieve this, existing methods commonly pre-render the target text at its intended locations to form glyph image that jointly specifies layout and glyph structure. However, this representation ties the resolution of each glyph to its target region, limiting fine-grained stroke guidance for small, densely packed, or structurally complex text. To tackle this, we propose GlyphAtlas, scene text synthesis framework that retains the glyph image as spatial anchor and supplies fine-grained stroke references through dedicated glyph atlas. The atlas renders and encodes each character or word in separate cell, decoupling reference resolution from target-region size. We further introduce atlas-anchored attention, which lets each text region retrieve stroke detail only from its corresponding atlas cells to refine the coarse glyph structure provided by the glyph image. This coarse-to-fine conditioning simplifies stroke rendering while preserving explicit layout correspondence. We optimize GlyphAtlas with mask-weighted flow matching objective and timestep-weighted text perceptual loss for writing accuracy and background consistency. Random resolution augmentation of atlas cells supports adjustable atlas budgets at inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GlyphAtlas achieves stroke-accurate synthesi"

First, miscalibration is structured by task rather than language: on parallel reading and topic items in 26 languages the confidence gap varies about as much as sampling noise, while across sources it spans 0.07 to +0.09, with knowledge tasks overconfident and acceptability judgements underconfident at all three sizes, so one fitted temperature cannot correct both. Second, the share of decisions that can be automated at 5% error budget follows chance-corrected skill (Spearman 0.97 over 33 source and model pairs) more closely than raw accuracy (0.91). Text:"drifting-distillation.github.io Figure 1: selection of 6 classes from ImageNet-1k [12] distilled to 10 images per class via drifting with DINOv3 [53] features. Images seen here were initialized with random noise. Our method yields synthetic data that are high in both quality and diversity by virtue of the attractive and repulsive forces of our drifting loss.The task of dataset distillation concerns the learning of small set of synthetic samples that can serve as an effective replacement for large real dataset when used for downstream tasks. While many solutions have been proposed since the problems inception, most of them primarily seek to optimize the distilled data with respect to classifier. In this work, we propose new distillation method that learns set of samples such that they directly approximate the per-class data distribution. Specifically, we drift our distilled data in the latent space of feature extractor such that they move both toward the real data and away from each other without using classification objective. We find that our drifted data cover the real distribution significantly better than existing methods while still serving as powerful training samples for classification. Furthermore, we showcase our methods use as visualization tool for better interpreting models and extend it beyond images to other modalities. Our work advances the frontier of dataset distillation, offering novel synthesis method and proposing distribution-based metrics as complementary evaluation. More broadly, our findings help address the growing need to understand both data and models by uncovering more about how they interact. 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026).Dataset distillation takes the classical problem of coreset selection [25] (choosing representative subset of large dataset) and extends it by allowing for the learning of set of optimal samples rather than restricting to selection of real data points. Since the tasks proposal in the titular paper [65], dataset distillation solutions have focused on the optimization of synthetic samples for the purpose of training image classification models. Be it via gradient- [74, 76], trajectory- [4, 10, 24], or featurematching [35, 62, 75] or directly maximizing classifiers confidence [8, 9, 36, 49, 56, 71, 72, 78], distillation methods have traditionally indexed heavily toward the classification objective, often paying less attention to other potentially desirable attributes of tiny synthetic dataset. Data distilled with respect to decision boundary inherit the classifiers biases, possibly limiting the datas usability for other tasks or generalization to other models. In this work, we aim to distill dataset with the explicit goal of best approximating the real data distribution. Inspired by recent work in generative modeling [13], we learn our synthetic data via drifting such that each synthetic sample is attracted toward each real sample while simultaneously being repelled from every other synthetic sample. The competing positive and negative forces encourage the distilled data to spread across the real distribution in way that promotes both coverage and diversity. As handful of images alone cannot adequately approximate the real distribution, we instead drift many augmentations of each synthetic image at time such that the distilled images, together with their augmentations, best cover the target distribution (Figure 2). We find that this drifting method yields qualitatively more diverse and higher-quality images than existing methods (Figures 1 and 4). Despite being distilled without classification objective, our images still serve as superior data for training such classifiers from scratch: the traditional dataset distillation benchmark (Sec. 4.1). Using MoCo-v3 [7] for the drifting feature space, our method reaches over 40% and 52% test accuracy when training ResNet-18 [26] from scratch with fixed soft labels after distilling ImageNet-1k [12] down to just 10 and 50 images per class, respectively. We further evaluate the distilled data on set of feature-space metrics (coverage [41] along with nearest-neighbor and linear probe classification) where our method continues to match or outperform existing methods (Sec. 4.2). Our method also offers some model interpretability insights (Sec. 4.3), generalizes to other modalities (Sec. 4.4), and even serves as an avenue for art (Sec. 4.5). Code and distilled datasets are available on our project page: drifting-distillation.github.io.Dataset Distillation. The concept of coreset selection [25], or finding representative subset of large dataset, has been thoroughly researched over many decades [2, 20, 37, 44, 46, 48, 57, 66, 69]. The idea of training model to competitive performance using only fraction of the data is attractive for many reasons, the most promising being the potential efficiency gains. More recently, dataset distillation [65], the learning of small, representative dataset, has gained favor, as the direct optimization of set of data points allows for more expressive and content-rich examples than being constrained to selection of real samples. For several years, gradientand trajectory-matching methods [3, 4, 10, 24, 65, 74, 76] yielded the best performance on classification-based dataset distillation tasks. However, these expensive bi-level optimization methods fail to scale to realistic models (ResNet [26], ViT [16], etc.) and datasets (e.g., ImageNet-1k [12]). In contrast, the relatively cheap, often task-agnostic, distribution-matching methods [62, 75] scale computationally to large models and data but perform poorly at that scale. Large-scale distillation began with the Squeeze, Recover, and Relabel [72] paradigm, which adopts the DeepInversion [70] objective: DeepDream-style [39] model inversion that adds BatchNorm-statistic matching to the cross-entropy minimization with respect to pre-trained classifier. Many follow-up works have since refined this method [9, 36, 49, 50, 71, 73]. These methods were the first to perform competitively on realistic models and datasets, but the distilled data are effective mainly for knowledge distillation [27] rather than from-scratch training. Some methods address the homogeneity issues that come with the classification objective via diversity-promoting regularizers [17, 38, 40, 51]. related line of work instead selects (and then optionally further optimizes) real image patches by scoring them with pre-tr"

Abstract An end-to-end flow-matching framework generates joint portfolio allocations from raw multi-asset market histories via a cross-asset flow Transformer and a two-stage imitation and group-relative reinforcement learning procedure, improving risk-adjusted returns on cryptocurrency perpetual futures.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Real-world portfolio management requires converting noisy market observations into coordinated, simultaneous allocations across multiple assets under shared capital constraints. Advances in forecasting models and alpha discovery help identify opportunities. However, traditional pipelines often decouple signal forecasting from portfolio construction, potentially misaligning learning with allocation and execution objectives. We present AllocFlow, an end-to-end flow-matching framework that directly generates joint portfolio allocations from raw multi-asset market histories. To capture dependencies among portfolio positions, AllocFlow employs a cross-asset flow Transformer conditioned on temporal market representations to generate continuous, signed position weights across all instruments jointly. To address the sample inefficiency and instability of learning continuous allocation policies from scratch, we establish a two-stage training scheme: strategy-supervised imitation shapes a structured distribution of candidate portfolios, followed by group-relative reinforcement learning (RL) that refines joint decisions using portfolio-level financial rewards and a frozen policy reference. On hourly cryptocurrency perpetual futures, RL refinement raises the annualized net return of the imitation policy from 27.79% to 51.38% and its Calmar ratio from 0.955 to 4.043, surpassing the teacher strategy on every metric, and AllocFlow reaches a Calmar ratio of 4.90 on six further months of unseen 2026 data without retraining.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract An end-to-end flow-matching framework generates joint portfolio allocations from raw multi-asset market histories via a cross-asset flow Transformer and a two-stage imitation and group-relative reinforcement learning procedure, improving risk-adjusted returns on cryptocurrency perpetual futures.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Real-world portfolio management requires converting noisy market observations into coordinated, simultaneous allocations across multiple assets under shared capital constraints. Advances in forecasting models and alpha discovery help identify opportunities. However, traditional pipelines often decouple signal forecasting from portfolio construction, potentially misaligning learning with allocation and execution objectives. We present AllocFlow, an end-to-end flow-matching framework that directly generates joint portfolio allocations from raw multi-asset market histories. To capture dependencies among portfolio positions, AllocFlow employs a cross-asset flow Transformer conditioned on temporal market representations to generate continuous, signed position weights across all instruments jointly. To address the sample inefficiency and instability of learning continuous allocation policies from scratch, we establish a two-stage training scheme: strategy-supervised imitation shapes a structured distribution of candidate portfolios, followed by group-relative reinforcement learning (RL) that refines joint decisions using portfolio-level financial rewards and a frozen policy reference. On hourly cryptocurrency perpetual futures, RL refinement raises the annualized net return of the imitation policy from 27.79% to 51.38% and its Calmar ratio from 0.955 to 4.043, surpassing the teacher strategy on every metric, and AllocFlow reaches a Calmar ratio of 4.90 on six further months of unseen 2026 data without retraining."

Abstract  We introduce Sage S1.5 Flash, a 320-billion-parameter general-purpose mixture-of-experts model designed to reason over scientific evidence and act in interactive environments. Sage Sci-Perception uses dedicated encoders for molecular graphs and biological sequences and represents spectra as ordered patches of measurements. Modality-specific projectors map these representations into the input space of a shared backbone, which processes them alongside text and images. To develop the model's agent capabilities, we construct verifiable environments for tool use, search, and coding, as well as specialized workflows involving machine learning experiments and scientific software. Task completion is checked against execution results, changes in environment state, or submitted artifacts. We evaluate Sage S1.5 Flash on 33 benchmarks across seven domains. In the primary comparison against five baselines, it ranks first on OfficeQA Pro (72.2), MLE-bench Lite (90.3 HumanRank), Harvey LAB, and SpreadsheetBench~2, and second on OSWorld-Verified (85.2), WebArena-Verified (67.6), and DeepResearch Bench~II (54.2). These results show particular strengths in using documentary evidence, producing verifiable workbooks, and carrying out machine learning tasks, alongside competitive performance in desktop and browser workflows that require the model to act on visual feedback.

Abstract  We present K2-Horizon, a fleet of six fully open language models, from a 0.9B dense model to a 375B-A23B MoE, built for general-purpose reasoning and agentic work across math and science, software engineering, deep research, office automation, and beyond. The models lead or are competitive with strong open models of their size on agentic and coding benchmarks. On the modeling side, to strengthen reasoning, we introduce Mixture-of-Value Attention (MoVA), applying MoE to attention value projections, and softplus attention gating, a more flexible, theoretically motivated gate. Our 36B-A4B model uses both and nearly matches a 32B dense model trained on the same data, with one-seventh the active parameters. We also introduce Uno, lossless diffusion adapters that speed up decoding by up to 2.2x without a separate draft model. Our release follows the LLM360 definition of open source for transparency and reproducibility: the full development history, including intermediate checkpoints from every training stage, pre-training data, training logs, and all training, evaluation, and infrastructure code, notably our production-scale xLLM framework for dense and MoE training and the RL360 reinforcement-learning stack. Beyond the data itself, we release and document in detail every pipeline that built it, the part of model development that open releases most rarely disclose. These pipelines produced roughly 10T synthetic pre-training tokens, showing that synthetic data can be a major ingredient of pre-training at scale. Four of the models share one 22T-token pre-training corpus and recipe, revealing consistent scaling behavior and enabling controlled studies of scale and sparsity. All artifacts are available at https://ifm.ai/k2/

"Abstract  We present K2-Horizon, a fleet of six fully open language models, from a 0.9B dense model to a 375B-A23B MoE, built for general-purpose reasoning and agentic work across math and science, software engineering, deep research, office automation, and beyond. The models lead or are competitive with strong open models of their size on agentic and coding benchmarks. On the modeling side, to strengthen reasoning, we introduce Mixture-of-Value Attention (MoVA), applying MoE to attention value projections, and softplus attention gating, a more flexible, theoretically motivated gate. Our 36B-A4B model uses both and nearly matches a 32B dense model trained on the same data, with one-seventh the active parameters. We also introduce Uno, lossless diffusion adapters that speed up decoding by up to 2.2x without a separate draft model. Our release follows the LLM360 definition of open source for transparency and reproducibility: the full development history, including intermediate checkpoints from every training stage, pre-training data, training logs, and all training, evaluation, and infrastructure code, notably our production-scale xLLM framework for dense and MoE training and the RL360 reinforcement-learning stack. Beyond the data itself, we release and document in detail every pipeline that built it, the part of model development that open releases most rarely disclose. These pipelines produced roughly 10T synthetic pre-training tokens, showing that synthetic data can be a major ingredient of pre-training at scale. Four of the models share one 22T-token pre-training corpus and recipe, revealing consistent scaling behavior and enabling controlled studies of scale and sparsity. All artifacts are available at https://ifm.ai/k2/"

Abstract InclusionMed is an open medical AI evaluation initiative that audits and reproduces benchmarks across healthcare roles and capabilities, revealing that model rankings and inference costs vary by context and motivating broader, context-specific evaluation.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Advancing medical AI for inclusive healthcare requires evaluation across diverse healthcare roles, tasks, and care settings, but benchmark coverage remains uneven. We introduce InclusionMed, an open medical AI evaluation initiative for inclusive healthcare. The InclusionMed Atlas organizes benchmarks across three healthcare roles and three AI capabilities. For the first release, we selected four public benchmarks from 388 candidates through structured audits and independent reproduction. With InclusionMed-DxFiles, our private diagnostic reasoning benchmark, the Atlas contains five benchmarks covering three of its nine role--capability cells. We evaluated frontier large language models on the Atlas and aggregated their results into the InclusionMed Index, with overall scores ranging from 17.5 to 49.1 out of 100. The Index also reports scores by healthcare role and AI capability, showing that a model's rank varied with the role or capability considered. A within-benchmark analysis using task-level tags showed that the ten highest-ranked models in the Index all scored higher on patient communication than on clinical decision-making. Models with similar Index scores also differed substantially in estimated inference cost. These findings highlight the need for evaluation covering more diverse healthcare needs and for context-specific model selection. Through open collaboration and community contributions, InclusionMed seeks to ensure that diverse healthcare needs worldwide shape how medical AI progress is defined and measured.

[17.11.2026 14:06] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. 'emoji': emoji that will reflect the theme of an article somehow, only one emoji. 'title': a slogan of a main idea of the article in Russian. Return only JSON and nothing else.

Abstract  Tool-using agents act on email, files, and payments, so an instruction hidden in a web page or a shared document can make an agent send data or money to an attacker. Many runtime defenses put a language model on the decision that lets an action through, so the decision depends on a model that may read the attacker's text and adds model calls to every step. Provenance defenses keep the model off the final check but need a model to plan or route each task first. Where an action goes can instead be read from the tools' typed returns: an email's sender or a payment's account arrives in a typed field, while injected instructions arrive as free text. SPILLWAY turns this observation into a deterministic floor with no per-task plan. The check admits a call when each target value that appears in tool output but not in the user's task was returned by a read-only tool in a typed field or is covered by a fixed delegation rule. A deterministic sanitizer removes instruction-like regions its fixed rules match before the model reads them and retains the removed spans as evidence, and a local judge may restore a refused call, never one whose target lies in the removed text. Across three backbones, and seven task suites on one of them, no standard templated injection redirects an action to an attacker's target under SPILLWAY, at the highest observed mean utility and composite scores (NRP, ERS) of the defenses we run under that attack. A forged chat template also produces a raw zero, and mean in-scope attack success under a released reinforcement-learning attacker is .007. SPILLWAY reaches this floor with no defense-triggered backbone re-run and almost no added model call. As personal AI agents act on users' email, files, and payments, SPILLWAY gives them an auditable refusal floor on where their actions go that keeps them useful and holds across backbones.
[17.11.2026 14:06] Response: {"desc": "Статья описывает SPILLWAY — детерминированную защиту для LLM-агентов от prompt injection, при которой злоумышленник прячет инструкции в веб-страницах или документах. Система проверяет адреса получателей действий по типизированным полям инструментов, а не по решению языковой модели, что не требует планирования задач заранее. Перед чтением моделью детерминированный санитайзер удаляет инструкциоподобные фрагменты и сохраняет их как доказательства, а локальный судья может восстановить отклонённый вызов. Эксперименты показывают почти нулевой успех атак при сохранении полезности агента и минимальных дополнительных вызовах модели.", "emoji": "🛡️", "title": "Агент не отправит деньги туда, куда велел злоумышленник, потому что адрес получателя проверяется детерминированно"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Tool-using agents act on email, files, and payments, so an instruction hidden in a web page or a shared document can make an agent send data or money to an attacker. Many runtime defenses put a language model on the decision that lets an action through, so the decision depends on a model that may read the attacker's text and adds model calls to every step. Provenance defenses keep the model off the final check but need a model to plan or route each task first. Where an action goes can instead be read from the tools' typed returns: an email's sender or a payment's account arrives in a typed field, while injected instructions arrive as free text. SPILLWAY turns this observation into a deterministic floor with no per-task plan. The check admits a call when each target value that appears in tool output but not in the user's task was returned by a read-only tool in a typed field or is covered by a fixed delegation rule. A deterministic sanitizer removes instruction-like regions its fixed rules match before the model reads them and retains the removed spans as evidence, and a local judge may restore a refused call, never one whose target lies in the removed text. Across three backbones, and seven task suites on one of them, no standard templated injection redirects an action to an attacker's target under SPILLWAY, at the highest observed mean utility and composite scores (NRP, ERS) of the defenses we run under that attack. A forged chat template also produces a raw zero, and mean in-scope attack success under a released reinforcement-learning attacker is .007. SPILLWAY reaches this floor with no defense-triggered backbone re-run and almost no added model call. As personal AI agents act on users' email, files, and payments, SPILLWAY gives them an auditable refusal floor on where their actions go that keeps them useful and holds across backbones."

Abstract  Frontier Vision-Language Models (VLMs) such as GPT-6 Astra have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in robot control, suggesting that broad pretraining provides knowledge valuable for physical action. Yet most Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies still rely on much smaller VLM backbones, leaving this potential largely untapped. We ask whether frontier-scale representations can provide a stronger foundation for robot learning. We introduce ScaleVLA, which connects a 552B DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash backbone to a trainable action DiT, and systematically compare VLMs from 2B to 552B under a shared training setup. We evaluate three fundamental capabilities of robot foundation models. In sample efficiency, just 10 demonstrations outperform a 2B backbone trained with 50 (87.8% vs. 85.6%). In generalization, LIBERO-PRO success reaches 45.1%, compared with at most 31.3% for smaller backbones. In precise manipulation, real-world charger insertion improves from 4/10 to 9/10 successes, with gains concentrated in contact-sensitive alignment. Despite its scale, ScaleVLA achieves 216 ms median latency per action chunk. Our findings suggest that frontier VLMs can serve not only as reasoning agents, but also as powerful representational foundations for efficient and generalizable robot policies.

Abstract A benchmark of realistic software engineering assignments reveals that current coding agents struggle to track requirements from clarification through implementation, verification, and handoff, completing few tasks despite partial test success.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash A coding agent working within a software organization must establish what to build, collaborate with colleagues, and verify what it delivers. We introduce SWE-CoWork, a benchmark comprising 101 development assignments across 101 real repositories, 11 programming languages, and 12 application domains. Each assignment places an agent in a simulated engineering organization with 26 colleagues who have distinct responsibilities, bounded knowledge, and controlled collaboration behavior. Starting from a brief handoff, agents must recover requirements distributed across colleagues and workplace records, reconcile conflicting evidence, and implement changes evaluated by private executable tests. We evaluate eleven models and find a substantial gap between partial implementation and complete task delivery. The two leading models satisfy approximately 63% of fail-to-pass (F2P) tests but complete fewer than 8% of assignments. Providing complete requirements upfront improves F2P by approximately 16 percentage points for Seed Evolving and GPT-6 Astra, yet leaves complete task success unchanged. Requirement-level trajectory analysis reveals failures throughout the delivery process: Claude-Opus-5.5 directs a mean of 93% of first questions about queried cards to their accountable owners across runs but obtains only 64% of colleague-held requirements. Deferred questions go unfollowed, implementations remain incomplete even after requirements are acquired, and 31% of requirements reported as completed fail their tests. These findings highlight sustained requirement tracking, from clarification through implementation, verification, and handoff, as a key challenge for reliable autonomous software engineering.

Abstract An AI reviewer trained by reinforcement learning to jointly maximize precision and recall of soundness review points outperforms frontier models and other training methods and transfers to other review domains.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash As AI conference submissions grow rapidly, AI reviewers are being considered as an alternative. However, simply imitating human reviewing is problematic because reviewing serves multiple purposes, including evaluating papers' soundness, interestingness, and clarity, the last two of which are subjective. In this work, we focus solely on evaluating soundness, the most important and objective criterion, and define the learning objective as jointly maximizing (1) precision, based on the soundness and substantiveness of review points, and (2) recall, the coverage of high-quality reference review points. Under this problem formulation, we construct a quality-labeled review point dataset, PaperSound-23K, using 1K ICLR submissions and 23K human-written and LLM-generated review points. Using this dataset, we train an AI reviewer model, PaperCritic, through reinforcement learning with the F1 score of precision and recall as the reward. Our experiments show that PaperCritic outperforms frontier models and models trained with other AI reviewer training methods on an in-domain test set. Surprisingly, learning to review ICLR submissions also transfers to reviewing Nature-family papers and even to out-of-domain reviewing tasks (e.g., code review, legal contract review, and feedback on story writing).

Модель обучается с помощью reinforcement learning, где награда — F1-мера, объединяющая precision (содержательность и корректность замечаний) и recall (полнота покрытия эталонных замечаний). Для обучения создан размеченный датасет PaperSound-23K из 1K статей ICLR и 23K замечаний, написанных людьми и LLM. Abstract VoxMel is a tokenizer-free autoregressive TTS framework that uses an invertible two-axis Haar transform to decompose mel spectrograms into Coarse-Mel and residual tiers, enabling stable high-fidelity generation with strong recognition and speaker-similarity results.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Continuous autoregressive TTS relies on an acoustic target that enables stable next-latent modeling while preserving fine-grained acoustic details. While existing approaches employ learned compressed representations (e.g., continuous VAE latents) to render prediction tractable, they rely on complex neural encoders that often cap generation fidelity. Conversely, training-free targets like mel-spectrograms eliminate encoder overhead, yet their potential for autoregressive generation remains largely underexplored. In this work, we propose VoxMel, a tokenizer-free framework for autoregressive TTS that makes stable mel modeling tractable through a fixed, exactly invertible two-axis Haar transform. The transform first decomposes each mel spectrogram along frequency into local averages and a frequency residual, then splits the averaged component along time into Coarse-Mel and a temporal residual. A language-model backbone with a flow-matching head autoregressively generates Coarse-Mel. Non-autoregressive residual tiers then predict the temporal residual from Coarse-Mel, followed by the frequency residual conditioned on the reconstructed Frequency-Averaged Mel. Neither residual is fed back into the autoregressive history. Trained on 100K hours of Emilia, the supervised 0.6B model obtains 1.51\% WER and 0.738 speaker similarity (SIM) on test-en, and 1.29\% CER and 0.755 SIM on test-zh. GRPO improves these results to 1.15\% WER / 0.753 SIM and 1.17\% CER / 0.776 SIM, respectively. We also distill the guided flow head into two- and four-step students, requiring 2 or 4 head evaluations per coarse frame. The four-step student retains competitive SIM and nearly matches the supervised model in WER/CER.
[17.11.2026 14:07] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет VoxMel — tokenizer-free фреймворк для авторегрессионного TTS, который моделирует mel-спектрограммы без обучаемых энкодеров. Используется фиксированное точно обратимое двухосевое преобразование Хаара: сначала по частоте выделяются локальные средние и частотный остаток, затем усреднённая часть делится по времени на Coarse-Mel и временной остаток. LLM-бэкбон с flow-matching головой авторегрессионно генерирует Coarse-Mel, а нерегрессионные residual-уровни восстанавливают детали, при этом остатки не подаются обратно в историю. Модель, обученная на 100K часов Emilia, достигает state-of-the-art по WER, CER и SIM, а дистилляция головы в 2–4 шага сохраняет высокое качество при меньших вычислениях.", "emoji": "🎙️", "title": "Точный синтез речи без энкодеров: раскладываем mel-спектрограмму по уровням"}

Abstract  Application optimization requires coding agents to discover useful changes and coordinate them across a complete program while preserving the required computation. We introduce Accelerate Bench, a benchmark of 32 CPU optimization tasks adapted from scientific applications and supercomputing competitions. Each task provides a working program and specifies the workload, correctness requirements, timing boundaries, and permitted edits, leaving agents to choose where and how to optimize. Public testing supports iterative development, and a separate verifier scores the final submission. On benchmark version 1.1, we evaluate 16 coding agents, each pairing a model with a harness. Their Score@1 ranges from 25.5% to 67.4%. Most of their programs are correct, and the size of the speedup separates them: the two leading agents are at least 2x faster on 22 or more of the 32 tasks, while the other fourteen reach 2x on 8 to 17 and leave 125 of their 412 correct outcomes below 1.25x. Controlled replays show why coordination across an application matters: identical source code runs between 0.29x and 2.12x depending only on build and runtime settings, and one code change is worth 1.01x or 6.4x depending on another. Accelerate Bench pairs executable workloads with agents' programs and trajectories to evaluate both their gains and the process behind them.

Abstract SALA aligns latent actions across human and robot videos by using language-based semantic similarities to form a shared latent action space that supervises embodied foundation model training and improves robot task performance.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Embodied foundation models hinge on diverse robot interaction data, which are costly to collect. Egocentric human videos offer a complementary source of experience, and latent action models (LAMs) provide a popular means of leveraging these videos for policy supervision. Compared with world-action models (WAMs), LAMs can offer greater training efficiency and potentially lower inference costs by avoiding explicit pixel prediction during policy learning and execution. However, most existing LAMs learn latent actions solely through video reconstruction, which can cause their representations to cluster by visual appearance rather than by the underlying actions. As a result, they struggle to align human actions with their robot counterparts. To bridge the gap, we introduce SALA, which emphasizes establishing the semantic correspondence for latent actions. Without requiring paired data, we use the language descriptions of unpaired human and robot clips to estimate semantic similarities and supervise cross-embodiment correspondence. Specifically, we translate these similarities into soft neighborhood structures that guide human latent actions toward semantically related robot latents, while robot action reconstruction grounds the latent space in control. The resulting shared latent action space provides a unified supervision signal for training an embodied foundation model on both human and robot videos. Notably, latent action supervision enables scaling along two axes: (1) pre-training on increasing amounts of general human video consistently reduces robot action prediction error on downstream tasks, and (2) post-training on increasing amounts of task-specific human video substantially improves real-world robot task success rates.

Семантическое сходство оценивается по языковым описаниям клипов, и на его основе строится мягкая структура соседства, направляющая человеческие латентные действия к семантически близким роботизированным. Реконструкция действий робота закрепляет общее латентное пространство в задачах управления, давая единый сигнал для обучения embodied foundation model. Эксперименты показывают, что предобучение на большом объёме человеческого видео снижает ошибку предсказания действий робота, а дообучение на задаче-специфичных видео заметно повышает успешность выполнения задач в реальном мире.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Учимся у людей через общий язык действий"}

Abstract A large language model can output single-chain protein structures from its internal residue states by invoking a pretrained structure head, with reduced long-chain accuracy and MMLU-Pro accuracy in the reported tests.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Biomolecular foundation models predict the structures of proteins and their complexes accurately, and large language models (LLMs) reason in natural language, but the two remain separate: the abilities of the former are not part of a general-purpose LLM, and the reasoning of the latter does not enter a structure prediction. Calpha aims at one general model that folds proteins and complexes, understands protein sequence and structure, and lets its language reasoning influence the structures it predicts. This report covers the first step, in which an LLM outputs single-chain protein structures. Qwen3.5-9B reads a sequence enriched with ESM2 residue features. It can respond in text or emit a token that invokes a pretrained SimpleFold structure head, which receives information about the protein only through Qwen's residue states. Under a fixed folding request, the head achieves mean TM-scores of 0.808 on the FoldBench-334 development set, 0.635 on CASP16 domains and 0.900 on a CAMEO-style PDB set. We compare these predictions with a baseline in which ESM2-3B encodes the sequence and a fine-tuned SimpleFold-3B head predicts the coordinates. The two systems also differ in trainable weights, training duration and crop length. For chains of 100--299 residues, all three paired confidence intervals include zero. At 300--600 residues, the mean differences are -0.044, -0.160 and -0.025, respectively. In a separate test, four English request types triggered folding on every evaluated example. Protein training reduced accuracy on a 350-item MMLU-Pro subset from 77.4% to 70.3%. An LLM can therefore output single-chain structures from its own states, with lower long-chain scores in the current comparisons and reduced accuracy on the evaluated MMLU-Pro subset. Complex prediction, structure understanding and the effect of language reasoning on the predicted coordinates remain future work.

Abstract  Streaming video generation must sustain quality beyond the training horizon while handling self-generated history and growing cache costs. To address these challenges, we present MovieRoll, a base-to-distilled framework that connects streaming fine-tuning, long-rollout distillation, and long-horizon inference. We first establish a robust multi-step base through Window-wise Teacher Forcing (WinTF), which jointly denoises chunks at progressive noise levels with bidirectional attention within each window and causal access to preceding history. To retain historical context efficiently, hierarchical KV-cache compression (HieraKV) exploits layer-wise attention patterns, retaining full history in shallow layers and sink--recent history in deeper layers. This reduces cache memory by up to 78% while improving generation quality. Building on this base, cumulative long tuning with distribution matching distillation (CuLT-DMD) holds parameters fixed throughout each long self-rollout and averages local gradients before updating. Each update therefore combines supervision across temporal positions while retaining only one local computation graph, enabling efficient long-rollout training of a few-step generator. To extend generation beyond the training horizon, KV recomputation bounds context for both models, while guidance annealing (AnnealCFG) reduces guidance toward the clean endpoint to stabilize MovieRoll-Base. Complementing these modeling advances, FBCA attention and PS-VAE decoding support efficient training and streaming output. On HeliosBench, MovieRoll-Base remains competitive at 30s after training on videos of up to 15s, and MovieRoll-Distilled achieves the highest total scores among the compared methods across single- and multi-event evaluations up to 60s, combining sustained naturalness with active motion.

Для стабильной базовой модели используется Window-wise Teacher Forcing, а для эффективного хранения истории применяется иерархическое сжатие KV-cache (HieraKV), которое уменьшает память кэша до 78%. Abstract Latent feedback, which feeds each token's top-layer state into the next token's input, improves compute and data efficiency over standard Transformers, especially when unique data is abundant relative to model size.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash When high-quality data runs out, a common practice is to train on it for several epochs, but each repeat is worth less than the last. Latent feedback, which feeds each token's top-layer state into the next token's input, is another way to spend that compute, yet it has not been tested against a vanilla Transformer that spends the same compute on more epochs. We run this test across a ladder of model sizes, unique data and epochs, tuning both models at every size and amount of unique data. Latent feedback makes every FLOP and every token count for more except when unique data is scarce relative to model size, a regime that today's models are far from. We find that latent feedback serves as an amplifier: with unique data fixed, it reaches a Transformer's loss with less compute; with epochs fixed, it reaches that loss with less unique data. Furthermore, both savings grow with unique data per parameter and with repetition, and downstream tasks agree. To go beyond our grid, we fit one scaling law to both models, in which latent feedback enters as a Transformer with more parameters; it predicts that both savings keep growing with unique data and repetition. Fitted on models from 60M to 271M parameters, the law predicts the losses at a held-out 778M size to within 0.3% and the gap between the two models to within 0.05%. At most, latent feedback saves 48% of the compute and 47% of the unique data. When data, not compute, is the limit, latent feedback is the better use of a FLOP for a model of a given size.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Latent feedback, which feeds each token's top-layer state into the next token's input, improves compute and data efficiency over standard Transformers, especially when unique data is abundant relative to model size.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash When high-quality data runs out, a common practice is to train on it for several epochs, but each repeat is worth less than the last. Latent feedback, which feeds each token's top-layer state into the next token's input, is another way to spend that compute, yet it has not been tested against a vanilla Transformer that spends the same compute on more epochs. We run this test across a ladder of model sizes, unique data and epochs, tuning both models at every size and amount of unique data. Latent feedback makes every FLOP and every token count for more except when unique data is scarce relative to model size, a regime that today's models are far from. We find that latent feedback serves as an amplifier: with unique data fixed, it reaches a Transformer's loss with less compute; with epochs fixed, it reaches that loss with less unique data. Furthermore, both savings grow with unique data per parameter and with repetition, and downstream tasks agree. To go beyond our grid, we fit one scaling law to both models, in which latent feedback enters as a Transformer with more parameters; it predicts that both savings keep growing with unique data and repetition. Fitted on models from 60M to 271M parameters, the law predicts the losses at a held-out 778M size to within 0.3% and the gap between the two models to within 0.05%. At most, latent feedback saves 48% of the compute and 47% of the unique data. When data, not compute, is the limit, latent feedback is the better use of a FLOP for a model of a given size."

Abstract A new benchmark, DominoBench, shows that current vision-language and multimodal models cannot reliably predict physical outcomes from still images unless the critical calculation and necessary quantities are provided.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Can a vision-language model look at a picture of a chain reaction and tell what will happen? Recent multimodal models describe scenes fluently and solve textbook physics problems, which suggests they should also predict how a scene of ramps, ledges, pendulums, seesaws and dominoes plays out once a ball is pushed. We tested this claim on 30 models from 10 families and reached a clear conclusion: unaided, no model predicts the outcome reliably. We built DominoBench, a benchmark of still images of 2D rigid-body scenes whose outcome a physics simulator computes. It holds 854 items in 427 minimal pairs, in which a change of one to three grid steps in one quantity decides whether one person survives, and every item comes with a four-rung hint ladder ending in the exact calculation that decides the outcome. Unaided, frontier models from Anthropic, Google and OpenAI are right about the person whose fate flips in at most 72% of items and answer both twins of a pair correctly in at most 34%. Pointing at the deciding step helps little, and exact geometry given as text barely helps, yet once the deciding calculation and the quantities it needs, including speeds that a still image cannot show, are written out, the same models reach 82-92% on the deciding person, while small open-weight models stay near chance. Physical prediction from a still image therefore fails at the quantitative step: the strongest models use the deciding quantity when they are given it, but do not produce it on their own. For models that plan or act in the physical world, a fluent account of a scene is thus no evidence of a correct prediction: such models must find and compute the deciding quantity unprompted, and DominoBench tests whether they do.

Тестировались 30 моделей, включая vision-language и мультимодальные LLM, и без подсказок ни одна не предсказывала результат надёжно: лучшие модели правильно угадывали судьбу персонажа лишь в 72% случаев. Abstract LoopFill reduces decoding latency for looped language models by combining multi-token suffix drafts and shallow-loop predictions into a single draft tree with full-depth verification that preserves the autoregressive output distribution.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Looped Transformers improve parameter efficiency by repeatedly applying the same Transformer layers. During autoregressive decoding, however, generating each token requires several sequential passes through these layers, leading to high decoding latency. Pipelined self-speculative methods use shallow-loop predictions to process tokens at different loops in the same batch, but each token must reach the drafting depth before the next can enter the pipeline. We introduce LoopFill, a training-free speculative decoding algorithm for looped LLM that combines multi-token suffix drafts with shallow-loop predictions in a single draft tree. Both drafters contribute to one tree, so a suffix lookup can immediately extend a shallow prediction and several future tokens can enter the pipeline together. The shared block computes the highest-scoring draft candidates at different loop depths in one batch, and full-depth verification preserves the model's autoregressive output distribution. Across seven benchmarks, LoopFill's average speedup over greedy autoregressive decoding is 5.85times on Ouro-2.6B and 5.13times on Huginn-3.5B. The speedups exceed those of LoopAhead, the strongest baseline, by \(40\%\) on Ouro-2.6B and \(25\%\) on Huginn-3.5B.
[17.11.2026 14:08] Response: {"desc": "LoopFill — это алгоритм спекулятивного декодирования без дополнительного обучения для looped LLM, то есть моделей, которые многократно применяют одни и те же слои Transformer. Он объединяет multi-token suffix drafts и предсказания с мелких проходов (shallow-loop predictions) в одно общее draft tree, поэтому несколько будущих токенов могут сразу войти в конвейер. Полная верификация на полной глубине сохраняет исходное распределение выходов модели, а значит качество генерации не меняется. На семи бенчмарках ускорение относительно жадного autoregressive декодирования составляет 5.85x для Ouro-2.6B и 5.13x для Huginn-3.5B, что на 40% и 25% выше, чем у сильнейшего базового метода LoopAhead.", "emoji": "🚀", "title": "Больше токенов за один проход: LoopFill ускоряет looped LLM без потери качества"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract LoopFill reduces decoding latency for looped language models by combining multi-token suffix drafts and shallow-loop predictions into a single draft tree with full-depth verification that preserves the autoregressive output distribution.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Looped Transformers improve parameter efficiency by repeatedly applying the same Transformer layers. During autoregressive decoding, however, generating each token requires several sequential passes through these layers, leading to high decoding latency. Pipelined self-speculative methods use shallow-loop predictions to process tokens at different loops in the same batch, but each token must reach the drafting depth before the next can enter the pipeline. We introduce LoopFill, a training-free speculative decoding algorithm for looped LLM that combines multi-token suffix drafts with shallow-loop predictions in a single draft tree. Both drafters contribute to one tree, so a suffix lookup can immediately extend a shallow prediction and several future tokens can enter the pipeline together. The shared block computes the highest-scoring draft candidates at different loop depths in one batch, and full-depth verification preserves the model's autoregressive output distribution. Across seven benchmarks, LoopFill's average speedup over greedy autoregressive decoding is 5.85times on Ouro-2.6B and 5.13times on Huginn-3.5B. The speedups exceed those of LoopAhead, the strongest baseline, by \(40\%\) on Ouro-2.6B and \(25\%\) on Huginn-3.5B."

Abstract A mixture-of-transformers architecture jointly predicts future visual states and actions, leveraging pretrained vision-language representations and egocentric videos to improve robot policy learning without action supervision. Abstract A human-in-the-loop failure-mining pipeline turns persistently unsolved grasps of a reinforcement-learning point-cloud policy into virtual-reality human demonstrations, improving hard-case success without reducing generalization to unseen objects.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Robots in homes, warehouses and farms must pick up objects they have never seen, usually without a 3D model or a category label. A grasping policy that works directly on the point cloud from a depth sensor needs only the geometry the robot observes, so it applies to any new object. Offline grasp generators such as Contact-GraspNet work this way, but they rank grasps by learned plausibility rather than by whether the grasp actually lifts the object. Reinforcement learning (RL) can optimize for that directly and make a point-cloud grasping policy much better on average, yet it leaves specific grasps persistently unsolved. We present a human-in-the-loop failure loop that targets those grasps: situations an RL policy keeps failing are mined automatically, sent to a virtual-reality application where a person picks the object up with their own hands, and returned to training as two grasp points. The person contributes only where to grasp; the robot chooses its own approach. In simulation, RL raises success on unseen objects from 55% with Contact-GraspNet to 83%. Adding 58 human demonstrations nearly doubles success on the hard cases RL kept failing, from 24% to 45%, and recovers performance that RL alone loses on similar cases that were never demonstrated, without reducing success on unseen objects.
[17.11.2026 14:08] Response: {"desc": "Статья описывает конвейер обучения захвата объектов роботом, где политика обучается с подкреплением (RL) напрямую на облаке точек от глубинного сенсора, без 3D-модели и без метки категории. Систематически находятся захваты, которые RL-политика постоянно не может выполнить, и отправляются в VR-приложение, где человек указывает две точки захвата своими руками. Эти демонстрации возвращаются в обучение, а сам подход к захвату выбирает робот. В симуляции RL поднимает успешность на невиданных объектах с 55% (Contact-GraspNet) до 83%, а добавление 58 человеческих демонстраций почти удваивает успех на сложных случаях — с 24% до 45%, не ухудшая обобщение.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Человек показывает, где хватать, а робот сам решает, как"}

Abstract Reformulating generation as progressive prediction of continuous perceptual representations improves visual understanding in unified multimodal models more than discrete-code or latent-flow objectives.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Unified multimodal models can support both visual understanding and generation, yet generative training does not necessarily improve understanding. We argue that this limitation may arise from a mismatch between the representation targeted by generation and the perceptual representation used for understanding. Conventional generation objectives primarily optimize synthesis-oriented spaces such as discrete visual codes or generative latents. We therefore introduce UMM-JEPA, which reformulates generation as progressive prediction of continuous visual-understanding representations. The model constructs a multi-scale representation hierarchy from coarse to fine, while pixel rendering is decoupled from representation pretraining and attached only afterward. Controlled comparisons show that the representation-prediction objective accounts for most of the understanding improvement, while a data-matched control leaves the group means nearly unchanged. UMM-JEPA reaches a 16-benchmark visual mean of 51.71, against 46.36 for the understanding-only Qwen-VL baseline, and the text mean also rises. Compared with discrete-code and latent-flow generation objectives, perceptual representation prediction yields stronger and more consistent transfer to understanding. Linear probes and visual-evidence interventions further reveal richer object and spatial structure and stronger use of image-specific information. These results suggest that generation can benefit understanding when it directly supervises the perceptual representations that understanding consumes.

Модель строит иерархию представлений от грубых к детальным, а рендеринг пикселей отделён от предобучения представлений и подключается лишь на последнем этапе. Контролируемые эксперименты показывают, что основной прирост качества понимания даёт именно цель предсказания представлений, а не просто объём данных, и средний результат на 16 бенчмарках вырос до 51.71 против 46.36 у базовой Qwen-VL. Abstract  Interactive video world models enable exploration through text and camera controls, yet direct object interaction requires finer control. Hands provide a natural interface, but translating continuous hand movements into coherent visual responses remains challenging because of complex hand-object dynamics and demanding real-time computation. To address these challenges, we introduce LynnReal-Hand, a hand-controlled video world model that unifies precise interaction modeling with efficient streaming generation. We curate 100K interaction clips spanning diverse real and synthetic environments and develop hand-aware supervision to improve fine-grained hand motions and object interactions. Building on this foundation, we learn a causal flow map for few-step generation and introduce region-aware score distillation that combines complementary video and image priors to refine interaction dynamics and visual appearance. To further reduce inference latency, we propose Ultra-Fast-VAE, a compact video decoder that preserves high reconstruction fidelity with substantially lower computational cost. Extensive evaluations on HandBench demonstrate superior hand-following accuracy and visual quality compared with existing approaches. Our full-sequence model achieves an FVD of 181.67 and a MANO Dice score of 93.02, while the three-step causal model maintains a MANO Dice score of 92.49. For real-time interaction, 540p streaming on a single H200 reaches 18.03 FPS with 741ms latency and an optimized peak GPU memory footprint of only 34.89GB. We will release the datasets, model weights, and the full training and inference code to support research on hand-controlled video world models.

Авторы собрали датасет из 100 тысяч клипов с взаимодействиями и ввели hand-aware supervision для точного моделирования мелкой моторики и динамики «рука-объект». Для быстрой генерации используется causal flow map с небольшим числом шагов и region-aware score distillation, а декодер Ultra-Fast-VAE снижает задержку при сохранении качества. На HandBench модель превосходит аналоги по точности следования за рукой и визуальному качеству и работает в реальном времени на одном H200 со стримингом 540p.", "emoji": "🖐️", "title": "Управляй миром жестами: генерация видео с точным контролем рук в реальном времени"}

Abstract OutfitterBench evaluates LLM agents managing a 200-day outdoor-equipment rental business under fixed capital and uncertain demand, revealing limitations in sustaining feasible commitments and reallocating resources.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash In many long-horizon settings, an agent's own choices create future constraints: committing finite capacity establishes future obligations and forecloses later opportunities. Sustaining performance therefore requires both keeping evolving plans feasible and comparing the value of resource allocations with the alternatives they displace. Recent benchmarks increasingly evaluate LLM agents in continuing operations, but give less attention to how agents keep their own commitments feasible while reassessing resource allocations as opportunities change. We introduce OutfitterBench, where agents manage an outdoor-equipment rental business for 200 days, purchasing from a 46-product catalog and handling roughly 1,100 reservation requests. Under a fixed capital ceiling, reservations and uncertain walk-in demand compete for reusable equipment whose availability changes over time. Experiments with seven frontier LLMs show that several agents underperform a simple rule-based baseline, while only the leading agent consistently surpasses it. Trajectory analysis shows that some agents apply known constraints inconsistently and make new decisions that conflict with existing commitments. Agents with more reliable fulfillment can still secure low returns from comparable commitments or delay resource reallocation. The leading agent better connects preparation with economic choices but also misses opportunities for more profitable asset use. These findings highlight limitations in sustaining coherent resource plans and reassessing their value over time. The benchmark code is publicly available at https://github.com/adminblackjack/OutfitterBench.

Агентам нужно выполнять бронирования и одновременно распределять переиспользуемое оборудование между резервациями и заказами «с улицы». Эксперименты с семью frontier LLM показали, что многие агенты уступают простой rule-based baseline, и лишь лучший агент стабильно её превосходит. Анализ траекторий выявил непоследовательное соблюдение ограничений и решения, противоречащие уже принятым обязательствам, а также запаздывание с перераспределением ресурсов.", "emoji": "🏕️", "title": "Агенты LLM теряют связность планов, когда ресурсы ограничены и обязательства накапливаются"}
