Abstract A framework uses coding agents and a feedback cascade to synthesize code-based policies that achieve long-horizon success and can be factored into a reusable goal compiler for zero-shot transfer to new tasks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Coding agents can synthesize and optimize code-based policies from simulator feedback, allowing the resulting program to act with zero inference model calls. But for long horizon tasks, two challenges arise: local improvements can hurt final success and switching to a new objective requires another expensive synthesis run. We propose a framework that first develops competence by refining the policy through a feedback cascade, moving from episode returns to hierarchical goals and belief updates which can be scored densely from simulator. In the Craftax game, a partially observed and procedurally generated environment, refinements via episode level feedback stall at <1% final-level arrival, whereas with the feedback cascade the optimized policy reaches the final stage 50% of time (28% final boss defeats). Furthermore, to make this competence reusable towards new goals, we factor the previous controller into an environment interpreter I (models, memory, skills) and a rule program p_0, and we show that coding agents can optimize a reusable goal compiler C_θ that maps structured tasks g into new rule programs p_g. Across five independent compiler searches trained only on short tasks, the resulting compilers successfully transfer zero-shot to longer tasks without any task-specific tuning nor inference-time LLM calls: continuation-scored feedback and a fixed machine with an editable program together turn a single-task competent policy into a reusable one.   Code at: https://github.com/vicgalle/craftax-program-policies
[17.11.2026 14:09] Response: {"desc": "Статья описывает фреймворк, в котором coding agents (агенты-программисты) синтезируют и улучшают кодовые политики на основе обратной связи от симулятора, так что итоговая программа работает без вызовов LLM. Для длинных горизонтов предложен каскад обратной связи: от наград эпизода к иерархическим целям и обновлению belief, что позволяет плотно оценивать прогресс и резко повышает долю успешного достижения финального уровня в среде Craftax. Далее среда разбивается на интерпретатор окружения и базовую rule-программу, а coding agents оптимизируют переиспользуемый goal compiler, который переводит структурированные задачи в новые программы правил. Компиляторы, обученные только на коротких задачах, без дообучения и без вызовов LLM переносятся на более длинные задачи в режиме zero-shot.", "emoji": "🧩", "title": "От одноразового решения к переиспользуемому компилятору целей"}

[17.11.2026 14:09] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract  Directional ablation removes a hidden state's component along a "behaviour direction" while a language model runs. It is the standard way to switch a behaviour such as refusal off, but it is erratic: on some models it barely fires, on others the text collapses into repetition. We show why, and fix it. At the last residual stream, ablation is exactly a logit bias: a fixed vocabulary footprint scaled by a gate, the cosine between the hidden state and the direction. The gate is measured from the origin, but last-layer states share a large common mode far from it, so a direction that overlaps the common mode fires on benign text and under-fires on its targets. Centered Ablation (CA) projects the common mode out of the direction, in closed form. Across 8 models, CA raises attack success by 29 and 46 points where the standard recipe under-fires, cuts collapse on Gemma-2-9B from 50\% to 1\%, and preserves 12 to 40 points more benign answers on high-overlap models, 39 to 46 more than the affine offset of prior work. Two numbers computed before generation predict, on models the analysis had not seen, which gain faithfulness and which gain attack success, and a collapse index marks when text degenerates. We also show that two standard safety judges score collapsed text in opposite directions, which can fabricate a 46-point effect.
[17.11.2026 14:09] Response: {"desc": "Статья рассматривает directional ablation — метод отключения поведения LLM (например, отказов) путём удаления компоненты скрытого состояния вдоль «поведенческого направления». Авторы показывают, что на последнем слое эта операция эквивалентна logit bias, а её нестабильность связана с общей модой (common mode) скрытых состояний, из-за которой направление захватывает нерелевантный текст. Предложенный метод Centered Ablation (CA) в замкнутом виде проецирует общую моду из направления, что повышает успешность атак и снижает деградацию текста на 8 моделях. Также вводятся предварительные метрики и индекс коллапса, а показано, что две стандартные оценки безопасности по-разному оценивают вырожденный текст.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Центрируй направление — и абляция перестанет сбоить"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Directional ablation removes a hidden state's component along a "behaviour direction" while a language model runs. It is the standard way to switch a behaviour such as refusal off, but it is erratic: on some models it barely fires, on others the text collapses into repetition. We show why, and fix it. At the last residual stream, ablation is exactly a logit bias: a fixed vocabulary footprint scaled by a gate, the cosine between the hidden state and the direction. The gate is measured from the origin, but last-layer states share a large common mode far from it, so a direction that overlaps the common mode fires on benign text and under-fires on its targets. Centered Ablation (CA) projects the common mode out of the direction, in closed form. Across 8 models, CA raises attack success by 29 and 46 points where the standard recipe under-fires, cuts collapse on Gemma-2-9B from 50\% to 1\%, and preserves 12 to 40 points more benign answers on high-overlap models, 39 to 46 more than the affine offset of prior work. Two numbers computed before generation predict, on models the analysis had not seen, which gain faithfulness and which gain attack success, and a collapse index marks when text degenerates. We also show that two standard safety judges score collapsed text in opposite directions, which can fabricate a 46-point effect."

["INFERENCE", "BENCHMARK"]
```

Other tags such as TRAINING, RLHF, or DATASET do not clearly apply.

Abstract GlyphAtlas uses a dedicated glyph atlas and atlas-anchored attention to provide fine-grained stroke references for scene text synthesis, improving stroke accuracy and zero-shot transfer.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Scene text synthesis requires stroke-accurate characters rendered in a specified layout, as well as visual coherence with the surrounding scene. To achieve this, existing methods commonly pre-render the target text at its intended locations to form a glyph image that jointly specifies layout and glyph structure. However, this representation ties the resolution of each glyph to its target region, limiting fine-grained stroke guidance for small, densely packed, or structurally complex text. To tackle this, we propose GlyphAtlas, a scene text synthesis framework that retains the glyph image as a spatial anchor and supplies fine-grained stroke references through a dedicated glyph atlas. The atlas renders and encodes each character or word in a separate cell, decoupling reference resolution from target-region size. We further introduce atlas-anchored attention, which lets each text region retrieve stroke detail only from its corresponding atlas cells to refine the coarse glyph structure provided by the glyph image. This coarse-to-fine conditioning simplifies stroke rendering while preserving explicit layout correspondence. We optimize GlyphAtlas with a mask-weighted flow matching objective and a timestep-weighted text perceptual loss for writing accuracy and background consistency. Random resolution augmentation of atlas cells supports adjustable atlas budgets at inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GlyphAtlas achieves stroke-accurate synthesis of small, dense, and structurally complex text, zero-shot transfer to languages unseen during task-specific training, and leading performance in scenes with many text regions. project page: https://fr0zencrane.github.io/project-glyphatlas/
[17.11.2026 14:09] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен GlyphAtlas — фреймворк для синтеза текста в сцене (scene text synthesis), который сохраняет глиф-изображение как пространственный якорь и добавляет отдельный glyph atlas с мелкими деталями штрихов для каждого символа или слова. Механизм atlas-anchored attention позволяет каждой текстовой области извлекать детали только из соответствующих ячеек атласа, уточняя грубую структуру глифа по схеме coarse-to-fine. Модель обучается с помощью mask-weighted flow matching и timestep-weighted text perceptual loss, а случайная аугментация разрешения ячеек позволяет регулировать бюджет атласа на этапе инференса. Эксперименты показывают высокую точность штрихов для мелкого и плотного текста, zero-shot перенос на языки, не виденные при обучении, и лучшие результаты в сценах с большим количеством текстовых областей.", "emoji": "🔤", "title": "Точные штрихи в каждой букве: от грубого глифа к детальному атласу"}

Abstract  Decision models read a document and return a probability for each of a fixed set of options instead of generating text. Several appeared in 2026, but none has published results outside English. We describe Zuse, three decision models (4B, 9B and 27B) that train a low-rank adapter and a pointer head on frozen open-weight bases, targeting 27 European languages, and use them to study how this design behaves across languages and tasks. On a broad European suite of 11 sources excluded from training, covering 9 task groups and 27 languages, a development set that also guided model selection, the 27B model reaches 87.5% accuracy with a pooled expected calibration error of 0.016, and 0.035 when weighted per source. Post-hoc analyses of these single-seed results give three findings. First, miscalibration is structured by task rather than language: on parallel reading and topic items in 26 languages the confidence gap varies about as much as sampling noise, while across sources it spans -0.07 to +0.09, with knowledge tasks overconfident and acceptability judgements underconfident at all three sizes, so one fitted temperature cannot correct both. Second, the share of decisions that can be automated at a 5% error budget follows chance-corrected skill (Spearman 0.97 over 33 source and model pairs) more closely than raw accuracy (0.91). Third, reach beyond the training languages is set by the base model: across 210 languages, changing the training run moved scores by at most about 2 points within a size, while languages outside the base's documented list trail by 23 to 28 points at every size. On English public suites Zuse is behind Jev and Kev, including Kev models on the same bases. We also report two pitfalls in building multi-source training data: label-word collision between topic schemes, and an injection augmentation that taught a shortcut. Zuse can be tried at https://bineric.com/zuse.
[17.11.2026 14:09] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет Zuse — три decision-модели (4B, 9B и 27B), которые читают документ и возвращают вероятности для фиксированного набора вариантов, а не генерируют текст. Модели обучены как LoRA-адаптеры и pointer head поверх замороженных open-weight baseline LLM и ориентированы на 27 европейских языков. На европейском наборе из 11 источников, не входивших в обучение, лучшая модель 27B достигает точности 87.5% и низкой калибровочной ошибки (ECE 0.016). Анализ показывает, что miscalibration определяется типом задачи, а не языком, а охват языков зависит от базовой модели.", "emoji": "🇪🇺", "title": "Zuse: Многоязычные decision-модели, которые знают, насколько они уверены"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Decision models read a document and return a probability for each of a fixed set of options instead of generating text. Several appeared in 2026, but none has published results outside English. We describe Zuse, three decision models (4B, 9B and 27B) that train a low-rank adapter and a pointer head on frozen open-weight bases, targeting 27 European languages, and use them to study how this design behaves across languages and tasks. On a broad European suite of 11 sources excluded from training, covering 9 task groups and 27 languages, a development set that also guided model selection, the 27B model reaches 87.5% accuracy with a pooled expected calibration error of 0.016, and 0.035 when weighted per source. Post-hoc analyses of these single-seed results give three findings. First, miscalibration is structured by task rather than language: on parallel reading and topic items in 26 languages the confidence gap varies about as much as sampling noise, while across sources it spans -0.07 to +0.09, with knowledge tasks overconfident and acceptability judgements underconfident at all three sizes, so one fitted temperature cannot correct both. Second, the share of decisions that can be automated at a 5% error budget follows chance-corrected skill (Spearman 0.97 over 33 source and model pairs) more closely than raw accuracy (0.91). Third, reach beyond the training languages is set by the base model: across 210 languages, changing the training run moved scores by at most about 2 points within a size, while languages outside the base's documented list trail by 23 to 28 points at every size. On English public suites Zuse is behind Jev and Kev, including Kev models on the same bases. We also report two pitfalls in building multi-source training data: label-word collision between topic schemes, and an injection augmentation that taught a shortcut. Zuse can be tried at https://bineric.com/zuse."

Abstract A dataset distillation method learns synthetic samples that approximate per-class data distributions in a feature extractor's latent space, improving distribution coverage and classification training while supporting visualization and other modalities.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash The task of dataset distillation concerns the learning of a small set of synthetic samples that can serve as an effective replacement for a large real dataset when used for downstream tasks. While many solutions have been proposed since the problem's inception, most of them primarily seek to optimize the distilled data with respect to a classifier. In this work, we propose a new distillation method that learns a set of samples such that they directly approximate the per-class data distribution. Specifically, we "drift" our distilled data in the latent space of a feature extractor such that they move both toward the real data and away from each other without using a classification objective. We find that our drifted data cover the real distribution significantly better than existing methods while still serving as powerful training samples for classification. Furthermore, we showcase our method's use as a visualization tool for better interpreting models and extend it beyond images to other modalities. Our work advances the frontier of dataset distillation, offering a novel synthesis method and proposing distribution-based metrics as a complementary evaluation. More broadly, our findings help address the growing need to understand both data and models by uncovering more about how they interact.

Образцы «дрейфуют» в латентном пространстве экстрактора признаков: они приближаются к реальным данным и отталкиваются друг от друга, без использования классификационной функции потерь. Abstract A reinforcement learning agent with a lightweight actor-critic architecture explores the Standard Model Effective Field Theory operator space to discover diverse high-quality theory hypotheses and improve global fits.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash In particle physics, discrepancies between experimental measurements and Standard Model (SM) predictions are commonly interpreted within new theoretical models. In this context, the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) provides a general framework encompassing a broad class of models. However, exploring the enormous space of possible SMEFT operators rapidly becomes a challenging combinatorial problem: many operators can contribute simultaneously to different observables, their effects are correlated, and distinct operator combinations can provide comparably good descriptions of the data. In this work, we formulate the exploration of the SMEFT operator space as a sequential decision-making problem using reinforcement learning (RL). Our RL agent employs a lightweight actor-critic architecture with approximately 40K trainable parameters in total. Rather than searching for a single best-fit theory, our method is designed to discover a diverse set of high-quality theory hypotheses. We test the method in a complex phenomenological environment comprising 132 observables across several sectors of particle physics, including 24 measurements that deviate from their SM predictions by more than 1.5σ. We show that the learned RL policy efficiently constructs multi-operator theories that substantially improve the global fit. Comparisons with independent random-subset and random-sequential searches demonstrate the ability of the RL policy to navigate this large combinatorial space. Our results establish RL as a systematic framework for exploring diverse patterns of new physics (NP) interactions within SMEFT.

Задача формулируется как последовательное принятие решений, а не поиск одной best-fit теории, и агент находит разнообразный набор качественных многооператорных гипотез. "Abstract A reinforcement learning agent with a lightweight actor-critic architecture explores the Standard Model Effective Field Theory operator space to discover diverse high-quality theory hypotheses and improve global fits.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash In particle physics, discrepancies between experimental measurements and Standard Model (SM) predictions are commonly interpreted within new theoretical models. In this context, the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) provides a general framework encompassing a broad class of models. However, exploring the enormous space of possible SMEFT operators rapidly becomes a challenging combinatorial problem: many operators can contribute simultaneously to different observables, their effects are correlated, and distinct operator combinations can provide comparably good descriptions of the data. In this work, we formulate the exploration of the SMEFT operator space as a sequential decision-making problem using reinforcement learning (RL). Our RL agent employs a lightweight actor-critic architecture with approximately 40K trainable parameters in total. Rather than searching for a single best-fit theory, our method is designed to discover a diverse set of high-quality theory hypotheses. We test the method in a complex phenomenological environment comprising 132 observables across several sectors of particle physics, including 24 measurements that deviate from their SM predictions by more than 1.5σ. We show that the learned RL policy efficiently constructs multi-operator theories that substantially improve the global fit. Comparisons with independent random-subset and random-sequential searches demonstrate the ability of the RL policy to navigate this large combinatorial space. Our results establish RL as a systematic framework for exploring diverse patterns of new physics (NP) interactions within SMEFT."

Abstract  Long chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning improves large vision--language models, but visual information often fades during generation, limiting long-horizon multimodal reasoning. Existing methods either re-inject vision at inference or train policies for stronger grounding, but where to intervene relies on perception heuristics rather than principled gain analysis, and how local visual influence propagates remains implicit. We study this problem from an information-theoretic standpoint and derive a lower bound on the downstream visual gain of a one-step intervention, which suggests two factors: local branching room (token entropy) and downstream visual propagation potential (suffix divergence from a vision-marginalized reference). Guided by this analysis, we propose reflection-anchor policy optimization (RAPO), a GRPO-based policy optimization method that selects high-entropy reflection anchors and optimizes a chain-masked finite-window KL surrogate for downstream visual dependence. Experiments on reasoning-intensive and general-domain benchmarks show that RAPO delivers substantial gains over strong baselines across multiple LVLM backbones. Mechanism analyses further indicate that reflection anchors are enriched for visually sensitive decision points and that RAPO increases contrastive visual-dependence signals along generated trajectories.

Авторы выводят с помощью теории информации нижнюю оценку визуального выигрыша от одношагового вмешательства, зависящую от энтропии токенов и дивергенции суффикса относительно визуально-маргинализированной ссылки.
